How to read this. The score column is wcag-nvim's headline number: a weighted APCA shortfall (content 60% · ui 25% · secondary 15%), aggregated per tier so it doesn't depend on how many groups a scheme defines. Tinted surfaces like CursorLine and Visual are audited but excluded from the score, since those are design choices rather than defects. Bands are relative to real-world schemes, which cluster around 63–86 — treat the score as a floor check and a regression signal, not a verdict.

The remaining columns show the pass rate at WCAG AA (4.5:1 body text / 3:1 UI) for a tier of highlight groups: content (buffer text), secondary (line numbers, comments, advisory), ui (splits, popups, non-text chrome). The bold number is the pass rate measured against the buffer background only; the smaller number after the slash is the pass rate across all background layers the group can appear over (popups, cursorline, etc).

WCAG 2.x contrast math is calibrated for dark text on a light background and is a known poor fit for the light-on-dark schemes most of these are — a colorscheme with low WCAG pass rates isn't necessarily hard to read. The APCA Lc values in the detail reports below (Lc 60 ≈ body text, Lc 45 ≈ large/bold) are the better guide; treat the pass-rate columns as a rough sort, not a verdict.

bg below is the theme's actual rendered background lightness (measured from its Normal highlight group), not the --background flag wcag-nvim happened to audit it under — some colorschemes ignore that flag entirely and render the same fixed palette either way, which otherwise mislabels light themes as dark and vice versa. When both requested-background audits come back byte-for-byte identical, the redundant row is dropped; you may still see a scheme listed twice if some other highlight group shifts with the flag even though the buffer background itself doesn't. The raw per-variant reports further down are wcag-nvim's untouched output, so their background= headers still show the requested flag, not this corrected label.
score: weak typical good strong
pass rate ≥90% 60–89% <60%

Overall ranking

Switch between repo averages and individual variants, restrict to a background, and rank by either the APCA headline score or the WCAG AA content pass rate. Content % shows buffer-background / all-layers, and in repo mode both columns are means over that repo's variants.
